Pierce Brosnan Endorses Callum Turner As Next James Bond As “He’s So Strong”; Josh O’Connor Reacts To Talk Around 007 Casting

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Two names rumored to take on the James Bond role next are Callum Turner and Josh O’Connor, and reactions to the idea of them playing 007 on the big screen are coming in.

Turner got a major endorsement from former James Bond star Pierce Brosnan and, in a new interview, shared his thoughts.

“All the men who are on the list now are actors I admire. Callum, Callum Turner is someone I’ve worked with, and he’s so strong,” Brosnan told Vanity Fair. “They all have a voice and a physique, and a presence on film. They all have longevity as actors, all of these young men.” Related Stories News Jack Lowden On James Bond & Gary Oldman's 007 Endorsement: "It's All Very Flattering" Casting Final James Bond Screen Tests To Take Place Over Next Week As Amazon Nears Naming The Next 007 - The Dish

Brosnan said he was “eager to see who is going to play this epic role of cinematic history,” later adding, “Whoever plays the role, I wish him the very best of life and strength and courage. You know, it’s hard to tell who it would be, but I eagerly await to hear the news and the celebration of the next James Bond.” Watch on Deadline

As far as Denis Villeneuve’s vision for the next iteration of James Bond, Brosnan said, “I think we are going to be blown away. I really do.”

“[Villeneuve] is such an iconoclastic director. His work is visionary, and his stealth and imagination of camera work and storytelling is so visceral and sensuous. It’s going to be great. It’s going to be truly magnificent,” he continued. “Of course, we all get sentimental and nostalgic, and you have to give it time and be patient, and it’s always such a delightful game. But Villeneuve is someone who I have great admiration for, and I look forward to seeing the work.”

O’Connor’s name has also floated around and was recently asked about the speculation of him taking on the role, telling British GQ, “Oh, Christ… It’s all very flattering. It’s nice to be mentioned — for any role — and I’m just not really going to add to the noise.”

“To be considered in that same sort of bracket is extraordinary,” adding that it’s “such an incredibly bizarre pinch me moment… even being uttered in that same sentence and being considered for something like that. But I am afraid there is nothing to say beyond that.”

Love Is Blind's Stew Debuts Beard Transformation After Popular Demand

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Love Is Blind star Scott "Stew" Stewart showed off a new look after Netflix fans campaigned for him to grow a beard following his short-lived engagement with Jacqueline "Jax" Guelli.

Scott "Stew" Stewart debuted a blinding glow-up.

The Love Is Blind star took the internet's advice by growing a beard to enhance his facial features.

"The sweet smell of victory," the 34-year-old captioned a TikTok showing off his suave new stubble, using audio of Michael Scott's famed line in The Office, "Well, well, well, how the turn tables turn."

And viewers were delighted to discover Stew's dapper new facial hair ahead of the season 11 reunion, set to air Nov. 8, after fan pages, hairstylists and med spas circulated images online that reimagined the reality star with a brawny beard to accentuate his jawline while complementing his eyes.

"The beard has arrived," one user wrote, to which Stew responded, “The people have spoken!!!"

And when another fan added, "Keep growing it out trust us," he quipped, "I never doubted you."

Stew is taking any styling tips in stride, responding to a user demanding he grow his beard longer by writing alongside a crying emoji, "Noted for the next one."

Stew's appearance first became a topic of conversation after his fiancé Jacqueline "Jax" Guelli called off their engagement seconds after their first face-to-face meeting despite building a connection behind a wall. When the engineer told Jax that he'd gained a reputation in the pods for resembling a Kennedy, she quipped, "More like Mitt Romney."

But the 31-year-old later clarified that she didn't ghost Stew on a whim despite how it appeared on the show.

"I just lost control of my emotions, and all I knew was to go home," Jax told E! News. "Truly it had nothing to do with him as a person."

She admitted that her cold feet began with the proposal in the pods, adding, "I was like, 'That didn't feel right.'"

Still, Jax moved forward with the relationship, hoping their in-person connection would take her by surprise.

"I wanted it to be like an aha moment, like, 'OK, now all is well. We have real time chemistry and connection,'" she explained. "It just was not there for me. And unfortunately, I panicked and I left."

Since then, the former couple has discussed the mishap "many times," the skincare founder said, including when she sent Stew a DM after leaving the show.

"It was super sincere," Stew told E! News. "She explained that it wasn't about me. It was about just the emotional overload of the previous couple weeks."

Read on for more behind-the-scenes of Love Is Blind, straight from the pod squad.

1. Are contestants paid to be on Love Is Blind? Former star Matthew Duliba said they are paid $5,000 to appear on the pod period of the show.

The real money is made in sponsorships afterward, as season two alums Deepti Vempati and Natalie Lee said they each made about $500,000 as influencers in under two years after their episodes aired in 2022.

2. Where do the cast members sleep while in the pods? According to Netflix, the contestants slept on cot beds in trailers next to the lounges during season one, but for season two they upgraded to hotel rooms to be more comfortable. They're escorted to and from their rooms by staff so they can't secretly see anyone.

"You're actually at a hotel," season four's Micah Lussier shared on TikTok in 2025. "That's where you're staying, and then the pods that you see is a set, so think of a big studio, and then the bathrooms are outside in trailers."

3. The reality stars don't get access to their phones starting in the pods until after they're back from their tropical vacation portion of filming.

Shayne Jansen shared of their vacations, "You don't have your phone or anything on you."

4. The pods portion of the experiment lasts about 10 days, with 40-50 hopeful singles serving as the initial cast. But over the course of the process, some singles are let go.

"There was a certain point where we did a whittling down of people just to focus internally on the people who were really connecting, to allow them more time," Love Is Blind creator Chris Coelen told E! News. "We ended up having maybe 20-25 people in the pods at that point."

5. The first dates between all of the singles were set up speed-dating style rotation, with everyone spending eight to 10 minutes with each person of the opposite sex. From then on, there were no limitations put on who you could talk to, when you could talk to them and for how long, as long as a production intermediary set it up as they were unable to communicate with each other outside of the pods.

6. According to Sal Perez from season two, the singles are given "talking points" and "questions" before heading into their dates, but they don't have to use them.

"There were kind of topics for the day," Kyle Abrams added in an interview with E! News. "It was good if there was a lull in conversation, you just refer to the little notebook and you can drum up conversation."

That being said, the show is far from scripted. As contestant Kelly Chase told Business Insider, "Although it might sound unbelievable (trust me, it was hard to believe myself), the show wasn't scripted for the cast."

7. As for what they eat on set? Kelly said that was one of the aspects of filming she enjoyed most, explaining that contestants are offered a wide range of options while in the pods.

"During the first 10 days of filming," she said, "we could choose from catered food that included options like grilled fish, chicken, steak, vegetables, pasta, rice, and fruit."

8. All the proposals happen on the same day, though some are shown earlier in the episodes to avoid watching them all back-to-back.

“Everyone gets engaged on that one day. And then the next day is the big reveal day," Kelly said, per Cosmo. "And then the couples separate and go back through their own door. You meet them [your partner] for 15 minutes—the person you just got engaged to—and then you don’t see them again for a few days. It’s crazy."

9. Where do the engagement rings come from?

"We supply them with the rings, and at that point, those rings are theirs," the creator told Entertainment Weekly in 2025. "They never have to give them back if things don't work out. They're their rings. And if they want to say that they paid for them, then that's up to them. They can say whatever they want."

10. Hosts Vanessa Lachey and Nick Lachey are no longer allowed to be at the reveals.

"We all used to be at the reveal," Vanessa told Parade in 2025. "So when they would come out on the red carpet, it was me, it was Nick, we had like the glam girls there, we had costume design, we had audio. We had everybody standing there."

But it ended up being "probably 50 people" in the room, which detracted from the romance.

"It got to a point where it got too loud," Vanessa added. "After that, they were like, 'No more people at the reveal. Just the cameras and the couple. This is their moment.'"

11. Hoping to see a few proposals, producers were not expecting more than five couples to get engaged in season one. So when two other couples (in addition to the six the show chose to follow) also ended the pod portion with an engagement, producers ultimately couldn't continue filming their journey.

"We felt like we wanted to tell diverse stories frankly," Coelen reasoned. "We weren't sure where the stories were going to go, everybody felt like they truly found the person they wanted to spend their life with and we had an abundance of story...we just didn't have time in the show to follow them, which is incredible to me."

12. One of those engaged couples was Rory Newbrough, who was often seen giving advice to the other men, and Danielle Drouin. They spent a week together in Miami as the other engaged couples went to Mexico to continue filming. But soon after they returned to Atlanta, they had a "very amicable" breakup when Danielle realized she still had feelings for another cast member, Matt Thomas. "She ended up wanting to pursue that," Rory explained to People. Danielle and Matt ultimately didn't last either.

13. The other unaired season one engagement was between Westley Baer, who was briefly shown discussing dating as a shorter man, and Lexie Skipper. Despite not continuing with the show, they dated for three months back in Atlanta. But Westley revealed to People that his career change caused their split. "I made a crazy decision to sell everything I owned, and I moved to Asia," he said.

Two season two couples—Caitlin McKee and Joey Miller and Kara Williams and Jason Beaumont—also got engaged, but didn't have their journeys filmed.

14. Are constants allowed to talk when there's not cameras around?

"In the pod stage, the answer is no," Micah said. "There's always cameras rolling in those pods. And when you're in the lounge, they don't want people talking if there's not cameras rolling, so it will be quiet on set. And that is because if you're talking about stuff, they can miss part of the storyline."

She said the experience in Mexico is similar.

"You won't fly together, you won't do anything off camera until they leave you alone in that room that first night," she said. "So, that's the first time that you get a talk off-camera."

But when they go home, there's more freedom to speak without being filmed.

"You have scheduled filming days, like every other day," she explained. "So of course, you get a ton of time off camera to get to know each other without them in your face."

"They pay for the dress," Micah said of the production team. "I remember when we went there were some dresses that were included and part of the budget, and we could choose from those. And there's a lot to choose from."

However, they also "had the option" to "upgrade" to a more expensive dress that they paid for. The season four star added, "To my knowledge, no one ever did that."

16. As for who paid for the weddings, a Netflix spokesperson revealed to Women's Health that production "supplies some of the basics, but because these are their real weddings, it's up to them as to how to spend their money." So the couples likely did have to cover some expense if they went over budget.

17. Despite some skepticism from viewers, the marriages are legit, with season one's Lauren Speed telling Refinery29, "This is a real legally binding marriage. This is not for TV. This is our life."

18. It's not random who goes first on the alter saying "I do" or "I don't," with Micah saying the production crew determines who goes first.

"It's not the officiant who decides," Micah revealed. "In my opinion, they will ask whoever they think is more likely to say 'yes' to go first, because then they get a two [person] answer."

Otherwise, she noted, "you don't have that chance of seeing the first person say 'yes' and having that shock."

19. So were the cast members obligated to make it to the altar? According to Coelen, they were not. "They certainly could choose to do whatever they wanted to do," he told E! News of the wedding day decision-making process. "Again, being there on the wedding day, I personally, knowing these people and their stories, I didn't know what was going to happen."

20. Then why do people wait until the altar to decide if they're going to say yes or no? While contestants aren't required to go through with the wedding day, there's a reason many do, even if they ultimately say no.

"In my experience, it wasn't like you have to get to the altar, like you must get to the altar," Micah shared on TikTok. "It was moreso being a part of the entire experience. The whole point of Love Is Blind is It's an experiment. And you wouldn't know if it's going to work or not unless you do the whole thing. So that was the thought process there."

21. Why do so few couples say “I do"? After only one pair of contestants tied the knot in season eight, Love Is Blind executive producer Ally Simpson provided insight into why it’s getting more rare for couples to make it past the altar, admitting that social media has made it challenging to recruit “nice men” for the experiment. She also noted that former contestant Chelsea Griffin joined the casting team to help mitigate the issue.

22. There were rumors online that Mark Cuevas, the youngest single man in the inaugural season, secretly had a girlfriend when he was on the show, which he later denied.

"I did not have a girlfriend the whole time," he said on the Be There in Five podcast. "I was single before [going on the show]. Now, being a single man, you're single, you're doing your thing. I'd never had a girlfriend before. Where was the time to be in a relationship during the experience?"

23. According to Kelly and Kenny Barnes, they never had any plans to actually get married at the end of filming season one. "We were adamant about we're not going to get married," Kenny told People. "And really, the engagement was just to extend the experiment. And we were both committed to that." And Kelly believed they were "going to continue dating, because that was communicated between us." That was until Kenny admitted on the wedding day he was not "emotionally available" to do so. "I felt very rejected," she admitted. "Like, 'That wasn't the plan. What the heck?'"

24. Jessica Batten, LIB season one's most polarizing cast member, claimed in an interview that she tried to quit the show but wasn't allowed to.

"I mean, I had to stay," she told EW. "I definitely had a conversation about leaving and I wasn't able to do that. My dog got sick too, and almost died during the show, I had so much other stuff going on."

She continued, "It was really frustrating because I kind of knew [Mark and I] weren't going to get there. I definitely had some conversations and attempted to leave, but I wasn't able to."

25. The season one weddings were actually filmed in the fall of 2018, meaning the two couples—Lauren and Cameron Hamilton, and Amber Pike and Matt Barnett—who actually decided to get married had to keep their relationships secret for 18 months before the show premiered.

26. Love Is Blind could go on and on for years, with Coelen telling E! News, "I'm thinking season 15" would be the last.

27. Did Carlton Morton look familiar? Well, it's because he's been on an ATL-set reality show before, popping up on The Real Housewives of Atlanta when he was working as Cynthia Bailey's assistant back in 2014. Bravo fans will remember he got into a heated argument with Kenya Moore, who had just joined the series.

28. Why does the show always have those gold cups? While fans have speculated the opaque cups help hide the amount of liquid in the glass to help with editing errors and cuts, Coelen said the goblets were chosen for aesthetics.

"When you turn on the show, you know it's our show," he told Variety. "It's a very authentic, really true following of these people's journeys, but I like the fact that we have this sort of connective tissue with that in a really light way, it's fun."

Deepti added to E! News, "We would always put all of our drinks in the gold goblets. It's just a symbolic thing for us."

Former Steelers QB Russell Wilson interviews for head coach job

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Former Pittsburgh Steelers quarterback Russell Wilson interviewed for the head coach opening at North Carolina State, per Adam Schefter.

"Former Super Bowl champion QB Russell Wilson interviewed today for the head coaching job at North Carolina State, where he played three seasons," Schefter reports. "NC State now has two former NFL greats who starred there, Wilson and Philip Rivers, as prominent head-coaching candidates."

Wilson is currently an analyst for CBS Sports on The NFL Today. He joined the Steelers in 2024 after two less-than-memorable years with the Denver Broncos. In one season with the Steelers, Wilson threw 16 touchdowns to five interceptions in 11 games for the Steelers, going 6-5 as a starter before the Steelers lost to the Baltimore Ravens in the wild card round. He then signed with the New York Giants, where he started three games before being benched for Jaxson Dart.

Wilson was a 10-time Pro Bowl selection, nine of which came from 2012-21 with the Seattle Seahawks, where he won a Super Bowl and went to another. He's 12th all-time in touchdown passes (353) and sixth all-time in passer rating (99.3).

XPENG Sales Soar!

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

XPENG had a fantabulous sales month in October. We can give you the numbers, but as you can see well enough in the chart above and below, the company’s sales soared to a whole new level last month.

Reaching 50,237 vehicle deliveries, that meant 22% sales growth month over month and 20% sales growth year over year. There were 41,256 XPENG vehicles delivered in September 2026 and 42,013 delivered in October 2025, the company’s previous monthly record. Will it be another year until XPENG beats its monthly sales record again, or is the October 2026 result just the beginning of more record sales in coming months?

Well, there are some reasons to expect even more growth. “In October 2026, the XPENG L05 was officially launched in the Chinese mainland, with deliveries set to begin on November 1, 2026,” XPENG shares. “On October 26, 2026, XPENG obtained a demonstration operation license to conduct Level 4 Robotaxi services in Guangzhou. The approval enables XPENG Robotaxi to advance from technology validation to real-world operational and business model validation, marking a key milestone toward the commercialization of XPENG’s Physical AI technologies.”

A big portion of this sales growth came from tremendous growth in sales outside of XPENG’s home market of China. “Overseas deliveries also reached an all-time monthly high of 13,326 units, up 166% year-over-year,” the company shares.

Overall, things are looking very bright for XPENG. And those EV sales are helping the planet and humanity as well. “XPENG’s electric vehicles delivered from January to October 2026 are expected to reduce life-cycle greenhouse gas emissions by more than 5.77 million tons compared to internal combustion engine vehicles, equivalent to the carbon absorbed by 95.43 million young trees over 10 years,” XPENG wrote in closing, and I’ll close with that highlight as well.

Oh, wait, I’ll also add that XPENG’s big spike in sales in October helped the company to shoot high above similar (but different) rival NIO in their long-term battle as the best selling smart EV startup.

NIO didn’t have its best month, but maybe it will surge again in November and/or December.

Beginner Tips And Tricks In Loftia

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

The world of Loftia is all about adorable sustainability, but it can be a little confusing when you first start out. Here are some tips and tricks you might need to get yourself fully immersed in this lighthearted, uplifting world.

Keep in mind that, as of the time of writing, Loftia is in its Early Access and as such does not contain its final features.

When you're just starting out, you don't need to worry too much about the aesthetics of your house. You'll want quick and easy access to your Project and Cooking Stations so that you can make what you need without having to run all the way to some obscure area first. I have my machines right inside my front door, sitting on opposite sides as you come in. This makes it easy to grab and go as I'm playing.

Learn how to get energy, upgrade your battery, and expand your solar farm in this complete energy Loftia guide.

Loftia City is the central hub of the game, full of resources and helpful NPCs you can talk to and get things from. It does, however, require you to jump in a balloon and move to a brand new area, which means waiting on loading screens.

Rather than having to hop back and forth between the city and your neighborhood constantly, try to plan ahead a little bit. Get all of your shopping and resource gathering done in one go as often as you can so that, when you do go back home, you've got everything you need to expand, plant, craft, and decorate for whatever project you're working on at the time.

You can expand your little island in Loftia to match your needs, whether that's giving yourself more room to craft, garden, or decorate. You do this by getting permits from Amani in Loftia City. It's a good idea to start with practical upgrades like a second energy station or garden bed before you focus on the upgrades that will give you more room to play with aesthetics. This lets you progress the game a bit more quickly and efficiently so you're not waiting around forever for things to grow or batteries to recharge.

Navigating Loftia City can be a little confusing at first; it's large, round, and pretty easy to run through blindly during the tutorial when you're looking for a specific NPC or location, meaning you don't get a great sense for the layout until you're exploring on your own later on.

While most of the major locations are marked, a couple of places (like the construction sight) aren't, which can make getting to them to go resource hunting a little more challenging. If you open your map (press M) and then right click, you can place a marker that will help you navigate toward your destination.

Markers will show up separately to your main quest arrows, so you don't have to worry about confusing the two.

As with any resource management game, you'll want to sort out your system for storing your resources and other materials early on. I like to have all of my raw materials in one chest, seeds and/or cooked items in another, crafted items in a different one, etc. You should also keep your chests relatively close to your machines if possible as well — again, having them by the front door is a good move, though you might also store them in front of the door as a part of your decorations.

I keep storage chests for food and seeds next to my Cooking Station, which is near where I laid out my garden beds for maximum convenience. Having a workflow makes life easier, even in gaming.

Cooking in Loftia is pretty easy, but it does involve a little clicker mini-game. You'll need to keep your mixing spoon in an ideal zone on a circle by clicking, not too quickly but not too slowly. If you can do that, you'll cook higher-quality food worth more Dew. If, however, you can't keep up with clicker games for whatever reason, Loftia has included an accessibility feature that turns off the cooking mini-game. I really appreciate this, as some folks may have limited dexterity and still want to enjoy the game; some folks might also just want to play the game in as low-stakes a mode as possible, and that's perfectly valid, too.

You can find the toggle for this by hitting Escape, then the gear for main settings, then tabbing to the Accessibility menu.

If you ever find yourself stuck or unsure what to do next, take a little time to chat with the NPCs around Loftia! Speaking to different characters can unlock additional quests, which gives you more activities to work toward when you're running low on things to do. Many of these quests also come with their own rewards, including new items, cosmetics, and other fun goodies to collect.

Even if you're more interested in decorating your home or dressing up your character than completing every task on your list, it's worth making the rounds and getting to know the locals. You never know what cute reward might be waiting behind a conversation!

Before buying a bunch of seeds, stop by Hazel's Seed Shop and check how much each crop sells for in the top-left corner of the page! The shop provides useful information about the selling prices of different crops, which makes it easier to plan your garden and decide which seeds are worth investing in.

If you're trying to make money efficiently, this is a great way to compare your options before spending your hard-earned resources. A bit of planning now can save you from filling your entire garden with crops that aren't particularly profitable!

Looking for Glass Coils, Scrap Wire, Clay, and more in Loftia? Here's where to find every resource and how to collect them.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Melat Kiros becomes the first Gen Z woman elected to Congress

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Politics  |  Read Source →

Melat Kiros looks down the line of assembled members of the Colorado Democratic Party during a unity rally put on by the party on July 2, 2026 in Denver. Timothy Hurst/Denver Post via Getty Images hide caption

Midterms coverage: Live results | What to know | Stories around the countryStay connected: NPR Politics Podcast | NPR Politics newsletter | NPR app

Democratic socialist Melat Kiros has won the race to represent Colorado's 1st Congressional District in the House, according to a race call by The Associated Press, marking the first time a Gen Z woman will serve in Congress.

Kiros, a 29-year-old Ethiopian immigrant and lawyer, cruised to victory Tuesday night in the reliably blue district, which includes most of Denver.

The win comes just months after she handily defeated 15-term incumbent Diana DeGette in the state's Democratic primary, a high-profile party upset where voter frustration with leaders in Washington, D.C., and calls for political change were at the forefront.

Kiros is one of several winning House candidates on Tuesday who were endorsed by the Democratic Socialists of America or its local chapters.

Many touted a shared core platform: championing issues aimed at expanding the social safety net for working Americans through policies like Medicare for All and universal childcare. They also advocated for more divisive changes, such as abolishing Immigration and Customs Enforcement and ending U.S. military aid to Israel.

Kiros' fierce criticism of Israel and the war in Gaza became a source of controversy on the campaign trail, sparking blowback from the independent candidate in her race, Shimon Blau, who accused her of espousing antisemitic rhetoric. Kiros has pushed back on that, saying her criticism rests with the state of Israel rather than the Jewish people.

Though the average age of a lawmaker in Congress has gotten younger in recent years, being a Gen Z member of Congress remains a rarity. Kiros will arrive on Capitol Hill at a moment when Democrats continue to confront calls from many of their voters for generational change.

In an interview with NPR in February, she spoke about the role of age in her race, arguing that many of her policy positions appeal to young voters in particular.

"Young people understand that we are in the fight of our lives," she said. "We need to have leaders that are going to be fighting for the kind of bold policies that actually get something done, and they're refusing to settle or wait for our turn or ask for permission."

Currently, just one sitting member of Congress is under the age of 30, Florida Democrat Maxwell Frost, who became the first Gen Zer elected to the House in 2022.

NPR does not offer or accept money for coverage or interviews. Anyone who makes such an offer or request is not an NPR employee and does not represent NPR.

How confident are you in the Bears and in Caleb Williams moving forward?

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Lifestyle  |  Read Source →

Welcome to SB Nation Reacts, a survey of fans across the NFL. Throughout the year, we ask questions of the most plugged-in Bears fans and fans across the country.

The Chicago Bears have won three straight games in the most impressive fashion imaginable. Caleb Williams is the 1st quarterback in Bears history to lead his team to 30+ points in three consecutive games with no turnovers. The Bears also never trailed in any of these three games, and that 3-game streak of scoring 30 points while never trailing is tied for the second-longest streak in franchise history. They went four in a row three times, all in the 1940s (1941, 1942, and 1948). Those teams were led by Hall of Fame quarterback Sid Luckman, who is the only quarterback to win a league MVP for the Bears.

There was some buzz around Williams as an MVP candidate before the season, and that was amplified following the Bears' 59-point explosion in Week 1, but the hamstring injury seemed to derail that because it kept him out of the next three games.

In his weekly NFL MVP Tracker at NFL.com, Kevin Patra wrote about the historical unlikelihood of Williams winning it.

In the post NFL-AFL merger era (since 1970) in non-strike-shortened seasons, only one player has missed three games and still won the NFL MVP award: Joe Montana in 1989. Cool Joe battled through various injuries that season, missing Weeks 6, 8, and 15, and portions of others.

While a long shot, the Bears did just beat last year's Super Bowl participants in back-to-back weeks on primetime. They also have three more stand-alone games on the schedule, including a third straight primetime affair this Sunday.

Patra also explained why Montana's performance in 1989 won it that year and how Caleb could have a similar path.

Despite missing three games, Montana was the best QB on the best team in the NFL, winning the MVP vote in a landslide (62 of 70 votes). The 49ers QB overcame the lack of counting stats by leading the NFL in all the efficiency categories: 70.2 percent completion rate, a 6.7 TD% rate, 53.7% success rate, 9.1 yards per attempt average, 270.8 yards per game, and set the then-record with a 112.4 passer rating — when 100 passer ratings were uncommon.

That's Williams' path to the MVP: Be the most efficient passer on the best team.

Caleb may not hit gaudy passing yard or touchdown numbers, but if all his other numbers are among the best in the NFL, while the Bears keep winning games, how could he not be a finalist?

Supermicro and Arista Networks crush expectations as AI infrastructure wins again

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Growing nervousness about spending on the artificial intelligence boom has made technology stocks increasingly volatile this year, but all it takes is one good quarter to reignite investor confidence, as Super Micro Computer Inc. and Arista Networks Inc. both showed today.

The companies are still relatively small fry in terms of the bigger AI picture, but as key data center infrastructure providers, they have shown consistently strong revenue growth over the last couple of years, and neither looks to be slowing down.

Indeed, Supermicro’s sales shot up by an incredible 298% in its latest quarter as it powered its way to yet another impressive revenue beat. The server maker reported fiscal 2027 first-quarter earnings before certain costs such as stock compensation of $1.81 per share on sales of $19.95 billion, cruising past Wall Street’s guidance of $1.23 per share in earnings and $17.35 billion in sales. After-hours, the company’s stock gained more than 6%.

Meanwhile, Arista delivered third-quarter earnings of $1.20 per share on revenue of $3.66 billion, up 58% from a year earlier, surpassing the Street’s target of $1.08 per share in earnings and $3.33 billion in revenue. Its stock moved higher too, and was up 7% in the late-trading session. The company is a major supplier of networking equipment for AI data centers, helping to link together massive clusters of graphics processing units and other high-powered chips.

Supermicro Chief Executive Charles Liang (pictured) said his company continues to sell its servers and storage solutions as fast as it can build them. “Supermicro’s transformation into a total AI and IT data center solution provider continues to accelerate,” he added.

Investors were looking for signs that Supermicro may face headwinds in the coming months, with the impact of rising component costs being one of their major concerns. But the company’s results seem to have reassured doubters that supply issues could impact its profitability. Notably, Supermicro said its gross margin rose to 12.8% in the quarter, higher than the 9.3% margin recorded in the same quarter one year ago.

Supermicro’s guidance for the current quarter was impressive too. The company said it’s looking for earnings of between $1.66 and $1.90 per share on sales of between $19.5 billion and $21.5 billion in its second quarter. That’s much higher than the Street’s forecast, which calls for earnings of $1.14 per share and sales of $18.1 billion.

Arista’s 58% revenue growth was the biggest year-over-year jump since the company went public back in 2014, and it could well break that record again if its guidance holds true. The company said it’s looking for fourth-quarter earnings of between $1.26 and $1.30 per share on sales of $3.95 billion at the midpoint of its range. That would represent growth of 59%. The guidance was better than expected, with Wall Street looking for earnings of just $1.16 per share on sales of $3.65 billion.

Arista Chairperson and Chief Executive Jayshree Ullal told analysts that even she was surprised by how well the company did in the prior quarter. “This strong momentum across customer sectors highlights our increased relevance for the generative and agentic AI era,” she said.

Georgia elects the nation's first Black female governor

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Politics  |  Read Source →

Keisha Lance Bottoms, Georgia Democratic gubernatorial candidate, arrives to speak at a news conference on Election Day, Tuesday, Nov. 3, 2026 in Smyrna, Ga. Brynn Anderson/AP Photo hide caption

Midterms coverage: Live results | What to know | Stories around the countryStay connected: NPR Politics Podcast | NPR Politics newsletter | NPR app

Keisha Lance Bottoms will be the first Black female governor elected in the country, after she won the Georgia governor's race, according to a race call by the Associated Press.

Bottoms' win marks the first time Georgia has elected a Democratic governor in more than 20 years, breaking the Republican hold on state government in Georgia. But she will likely be working with a Republican majority in at least one chamber of the legislature.

Bottoms, 56, defeated Republican Rick Jackson, a billionaire healthcare executive endorsed by President Trump. Trump won Georgia in 2024, but lost the state in 2020. Since then, Georgia has evolved from a state that consistently elects Republicans to federal and state office to a swing state with Democratic senators. But the governor's office and the legislature have remained solidly Republican.

Throughout her campaign, Bottoms made fully expanding Medicaid in the state, which would require legislative approval, a top campaign promise.

Before running for governor, Bottoms served as mayor of Atlanta for a single tumultuous term from 2018-2022, during which the city grappled with the COVID-19 pandemic and the social unrest seen in other major cities at that time. She declined to run for a second term and instead served as an adviser in the Biden administration. Her opponent used her record as mayor against her in attack ads, claiming she abandoned the city after allowing crime to fester — because Atlanta, like many other major cities, saw an increase in crime during the pandemic.

Bottoms harnessed frustration against Trump to propel her campaign. She promised to stand up to the Trump administration while in office, and to find relief for high prices. One of her proposals includes allowing local municipalities to eliminate the sales tax on groceries. She has also said she wants to eliminate the state income tax for public school teachers.

Data centers also emerged as a top campaign issue, as the state leads the nation in new data center construction. Bottoms said she backs a moratorium on new data centers.

"We need experts in this field to sit around the table and tell us and give us guidance as to how the state of Georgia should move forward," Bottoms said in October, during her only gubernatorial debate with Jackson.

Bottoms touted Trump's endorsement of Jackson throughout the campaign. Jackson aligned himself with Trump during the primaries, but later distanced himself from the president by removing references to him on his website and leaving Trump's name out of speeches on the campaign trail. Instead, he allied himself with Georgia's current governor, Brian Kemp, who endorsed Jackson after the primary.

Bottoms gained her own endorsements from several high-profile Democrats, including former presidents Joe Biden and Barack Obama. She also regularly campaigned with Sen. Jon Ossoff, one of Georgia's two Democratic U.S. senators.

Sarah Kallis is the politics reporter at Georgia Public Broadcasting.

Looking for a political match? Enter Senate Island.

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Apparently, having a type extends to tax policy as well.

Welcome to Senate Island, where finding a connection means comparing healthcare plans and the next recoupling happens at the polls. The parody website, inspired by the hit reality show Love Island, gives Senate candidates the reality-dating-show treatment, inviting visitors to size up their political connections before casting a ballot in the 2026 midterms. The states are villas, the candidates are islanders, and finding a match involves comparing views on healthcare and housing.

Created by Chicago-based art director and designer Anweih Cox, the site launched Oct. 26 and has since attracted more than 80,000 visitors and half a million page views, according to The 19th. (Cox had initially hoped for 1,000 page views.) It also, naturally, went viral on socials.

The inspiration came from watching Love Island USA and thinking about the enthusiasm fans bring to voting on the show's app, Cox said. If people could get that invested in a recoupling, perhaps the format could make researching an election feel a little more approachable.

Visitors begin by choosing their state, or "villa," then meeting the candidates hoping to couple up with voters. In Texas, for example, the lineup includes James Talarico, Ken Paxton, and Ted Brown, presented in swipeable profile cards with their names, ages, and AI-generated portraits. (Apparently, the villa dress code is a button-down and khakis.)

From there, the "Casa Amor" tab takes visitors to a side-by-side comparison of candidates' positions on education, healthcare, housing, AI, and the cost of living. In our Texas walkthrough, selecting education brought up Talarico's opposition to using public school funding to subsidize private schools alongside Paxton’s support for universal school choice. The summaries keep the dating-show language going, with Talarico's entry describing that funding arrangement as a "dealbreaker."

The policy summaries borrow the language of dating profiles. For example, Florida Democrat Angie Nixon's cost-of-living entry opens with an invitation to swipe right in response to frustration with price gouging, while Republican Ashley Moody's entry frames tax proposals as a love language.

After choosing a candidate, visitors reach voting resources, including links to check registration, find a polling place, and learn more about their ballot. (Choosing someone on the website doesn't cast an actual vote.)

"It's important because a lot of times people feel very intimidated," Cox told The 19th. "You have to go through all these different sites sometimes just to hunt down the information you’re looking for, and so just having that starting resource that is on the more playful side just makes it feel less intimidating."

The project has reached someone very familiar with running a villa. Love Island USA host Ariana Madix commented on Cox's TikTok, writing, "you are brilliant."

There's a 99% Chance That Democrats Retake the House, and History Says This Shift Will Have a Huge Impact on Stocks

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nearly a century of S&P 500 return data shows that a worst-case scenario is likely to unfold -- but this isn’t the complete story.

Throughout the day, Americans from across our great nation have headed to the polls or mailed in their ballots to make their voices heard. According to popular prediction market Kalshi, as of 8:50 p.m. ET, the odds have skyrocketed to better than 99% that Democrats will retake control of the House of Representatives. Regardless of what happens in the Senate, President Trump would be working with a divided Congress come January 2027.

Based on what history tells us, a split Congress can significantly alter annualized return expectations for the Dow Jones Industrial Average (^DJI -0.38%), S&P 500 (^GSPC +0.36%), and Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC +0.83%).

President Trump will likely be working with a divided Congress in the new year. Image source: Official White House Photo by Joyce N. Boghosian.

If prediction markets prove accurate and Democrats do retake one or both houses of Congress, President Donald Trump's second nonconsecutive term will mirror his first. He'll have had two years of full Republican congressional control in the first half of each term, and a divided Congress in the second half of both.

To some extent, congressional division is welcome by Wall Street. Political gridlock makes it virtually impossible to pass major tax and spending legislation. In other words, personal and corporate income tax law should remain unchanged for the foreseeable future.

DEMOCRATIC SWEEP? UBS SEES 50% ODDS. UBS assigns a 50% probability to Democrats winning both chambers, while prediction markets put Democratic control of the House at 94% and Senate at 77%. UBS warns a sweep could complicate Trump's fiscal agenda and 2027 debt-ceiling negotiations, while tariffs and AI investment remain largely insulated.

But a congressional shake-up does have its sticking points. For instance, debt-ceiling negotiations would be far more challenging. Additionally, Trump's desire to minimize the regulation of artificial intelligence would clash with Democratic lawmakers who favor much tougher oversight of the stock market's top catalyst.

However, the biggest worry for investors comes from nearly a century of historical return data. A few years ago, online retirement planning site Retirement Researcher examined the average annual return of the S&P 500 from 1926 through 2023 under four political scenarios, yielding one outlier:

Statistically, no political scenario has produced less desirable returns for stock investors than what's expected to unfold on Election Night. But this also isn't the complete story.

While nearly a century of data points to lower average annual returns for the Dow, S&P 500, and Nasdaq Composite during President Trump's final two years in the White House, investors are in better shape than this data suggests.

For starters, a 7.33% average annual return still (pardon the apropos pun) trumps the long-term average annual return of other major asset classes, such as commodities, real estate, and Treasury bonds.

These next six months are historically the best of the year, but they are even better in a midterm year. In fact, 19 for 19 times we've seen November - April higher when starting in a midterm year. Will this time be different? https://t.co/kebp03JxpR

Furthermore, Carson Group's Chief Market Strategist, Ryan Detrick, notes that the six months following midterm elections have historically been exceptionally generous to investors since 1950, with November higher 19 of 19 times. In fact, the S&P 500 hasn't been down in the six- or 12-month period following a midterm election over the last 75 years.

In other words, even though history shows that near-term returns may be tempered a bit as President Trump and a divided Congress learn to work together, the long-term outlook for the Dow Jones Industrial Average, S&P 500, and Nasdaq Composite remains as bright as ever.

US midterms election 2026 live: Crypto-backed Andy Barr wins Kentucky Senate seat

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Follow live coverage of the 2026 US midterm elections, including key congressional races, crypto-backed candidates and what the results could mean for digital asset regulation.

Within the next few hours, we will begin to learn whether President Donald Trump’s Republican Party has managed to retain control of the US Congress, determining his ability to advance his legislative agenda during the final two years of his term.

Follow the latest developments in congressional races attracting crypto industry spending, election results and reactions from prediction markets. Cointelegraph is using live election results data from the Associated Press. Updates appear in reverse chronological order.

Republican Representative Andy Barr has won Kentucky’s US Senate race, sending a supporter of the CLARITY and GENIUS Acts to the upper chamber.

Fairshake-affiliated political action committees spent more than $7 million supporting Barr in the Republican primary. Barr has also introduced legislation aimed at protecting lawful businesses, including crypto firms, from debanking.

Republican Representative Harriet Hageman has won Wyoming’s US Senate race against Democrat James Byrd, succeeding retiring crypto advocate Senator Cynthia Lummis.

Lummis, who chairs the Senate Banking Subcommittee on Digital Assets, sponsored the BITCOIN Act of 2025 to establish a US strategic Bitcoin reserve and has supported the CLARITY and GENIUS Acts.

Hageman has also backed cryptocurrency legislation, voting to pass the CLARITY and GENIUS Acts in 2025 and the FIT21 market structure bill in 2024. She also cosponsored legislation seeking to prohibit the Federal Reserve from issuing a central bank digital currency, according to Stand With Crypto. The advocacy group lists no documented position on cryptocurrency for Byrd.

Kalshi and Polymarket traders now give Democrats a 99% chance of winning Senate control and price House control at nearly 100%. It came as Democrats flipped North Carolina’s Senate seat, while holding Georgia and New Hampshire.

Prediction markets increasingly favored Democrats taking control of both chambers of Congress on Tuesday evening.

As of 8:05 pm ET, the Blockchain Association’s election dashboard showed Democratic Senate-control odds at 94% on Kalshi and 93% on Polymarket, up from 75% and 75.5%, respectively, earlier in the day. Democratic House-control odds stood at 98% and 99%.

It comes as Democrats and allied independents have secured 42 Senate seats to Republicans’ 31.

Massachusetts Senator Ed Markey and Delaware Senator Chris Coons, both Democrats, win reelection, according to the Associated Press.

Markey voted against the GENIUS stablecoin bill in 2025, while Coons has criticized President Donald Trump’s crypto ventures, calling his latest memecoin-holder event “corruption.”

Democratic Sen. Jon Ossoff has won reelection in Georgia, beating Republican Representative Mike Collins.

Ossoff expressed support for impeaching Trump in April 2025 over plans to host a private dinner for top holders of the president’s TRUMP memecoin, arguing that the arrangement amounted to selling access to the presidency.

Maylea Ma, deputy general counsel at 1inch, said she expects federal agencies to drive the next phase of crypto regulation.

The election could reshape the path forward for the stalled CLARITY Act, but Ma said “real movement” in US digital-asset regulation over the next few years will come from the US Securities and Exchange Commission, Commodity Futures Trading Commission and Treasury, rather than Congress.

Ma said those agencies were increasingly engaging with crypto projects. However, agency engagement does not provide the stability the industry had hoped to secure through legislation.

“The long-term goal, of course, remains a statute that cannot be reversed every time a government changes,” said Ma.

Democratic Rep. Debbie Wasserman Schultz wins Florida’s 20th Congressional District. Wasserman Schultz voted against the CLARITY crypto market structure bill when it passed the House in July 2025.

Democratic Senator Mark Warner has won re-election in Virginia, defeating Republican challenger Bert Mizusawa. Warner was widely favored to win.

Warner voted against advancing the CLARITY Act in September, arguing that crypto legislation needs safeguards against President Donald Trump personally benefiting from the industry. Warner supports establishing federal rules for digital assets, with his opposition to the bill focused on its ethics protections.

The first polls in the US midterm elections closed at 6 pm ET in parts of Indiana and Kentucky. The remaining polls in both states close at 7 pm ET.

Polls also close at 7 pm ET in Georgia and Virginia, followed by Ohio at 7:30 pm ET, where Republican Sen. Jon Husted faces Democrat Sherrod Brown in a race drawing major crypto industry spending.

More than 57 million Americans had cast ballots as of Monday, with early voting surpassing 2022 levels in several closely watched states, according to the Associated Press.

Current balance of power in the US House and Senate. Source: Blockchain Association

In Ohio, Republican Sen. Jon Husted faces Democrat Sherrod Brown, a longtime crypto critic, in a race that has attracted approximately $63 million in crypto industry spending. That includes about $56 million from Fairshake and $7 million from the Winklevoss-backed Digital Freedom Fund, with both groups spending to support Husted or oppose Brown.

Crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto characterizes Brown as strongly opposed to cryptocurrency, citing 17 statements and his 2024 vote against overturning the SEC’s SAB 121 crypto custody accounting guidance.

Brown was also targeted by crypto industry spending in 2024, when Fairshake affiliate Defend American Jobs spent more than $40 million supporting Republican Bernie Moreno, who defeated Brown in that year’s Senate election.

Republican Sen. Pete Ricketts faces independent challenger Dan Osborn in Nebraska, where crypto political action committee Fairshake had spent nearly $11 million supporting Ricketts, according to an Oct. 27 report by the Washington Examiner.

More recent spending data shows Fairshake-affiliated crypto super PAC Defend American Jobs spent approximately $13.5 million opposing Osborn, according to Federal Election Commission data compiled by Bankrolled as of Nov. 3.

Ricketts, a member of the Senate Banking Committee, has supported cryptocurrency legislation including the CLARITY Act and GENIUS Act. He voted to advance the CLARITY Act out of committee in May and supported an unsuccessful Senate procedural vote on the legislation in September, according to crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto.

In Texas, Republican Ken Paxton faces Democrat James Talarico, with the Winklevoss-backed Digital Freedom Fund supporting Paxton, despite both candidates receiving A ratings from crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto.

Paxton has called for the passage of the CLARITY Act, arguing that clear regulations are necessary to maintain US competitiveness in digital assets. In a June statement, he said the country must become the “crypto capital of the world” and support innovation and growth.

Talarico has similarly called for clear regulations that protect consumers while allowing innovation. As a Texas state representative, he also voted in 2025 to establish a state strategic Bitcoin reserve, according to legislative records cited by Stand With Crypto.

In Wyoming, Republican Rep. Harriet Hageman faces Democrat James Byrd for the seat being vacated by Sen. Cynthia Lummis, a leading cryptocurrency advocate who is not seeking reelection.

Lummis, who chairs the Senate Banking Subcommittee on Digital Assets, sponsored the BITCOIN Act of 2025 to establish a US strategic Bitcoin reserve and has supported the CLARITY and GENIUS Acts.

Hageman has also backed cryptocurrency legislation, voting to pass the CLARITY and GENIUS Acts in 2025 and the FIT21 market structure bill in 2024. She also cosponsored legislation seeking to prohibit the Federal Reserve from issuing a central bank digital currency, according to Stand With Crypto. The advocacy group lists no documented position on cryptocurrency for Byrd.

Maine senator Susan Collins was one of four Republicans to oppose advancing the CLARITY Act in September, expressing concerns over its impact on bank deposits. She now faces Democrat Troy Jackson to defend her seat.

Unlike Collins, Jackson has expressed support for clear cryptocurrency legislation in the run-up to the election. According to Stand with Crypto, which rates Jackson as “very pro crypto”, he called to end the debanking of “lawful crypto businesses, and ensure Americans’ right to self-custody digital assets.”

Fairshake, backed by Coinbase, Ripple and Andreessen Horowitz, has led the spending through its affiliated super PACs, Defend American Jobs and Protect Progress. In early October, the group announced support for 32 incumbent House candidates, including 19 Republicans and 13 Democrats, all of whom voted in favor of the CLARITY Act in 2025.

Other crypto-aligned groups include the Digital Freedom Fund, backed by Tyler and Cameron Winklevoss, the twin co-founders of crypto exchange Gemini, and Fellowship PAC, which has supported candidates from both parties.

Josh Jacobs needs to display more (or any) contrition

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

In May 2026, Packers running back Josh Jacobs crossed a line that should never be crossed. And neither he nor his representatives are acting accordingly in what should be an effort to seek redemption.

He pleaded no contest to battery of a woman. He’s fighting in court to keep the video secret. He’s responding to additional allegations through a strident statement from his lawyers attacking the accusers.

The most tone-deaf gesture of all came last Thursday night, when he celebrated a touchdown by pretending to wear, and to be released from, handcuffs.

His implausible explanation, which accused Prime Video of not showing enough of the celebration and attacked the media for interpreting the images with their lyin’ eyes, was contradicted by the full video of the event.

That’s not the way to play this. His no-contest plea is equivalent to saying, “I did it.” He should be contrite. He should be apologetic. He should be seeking redemption.

The Packers continue to stand behind him, primarily because he’s the best running back on the roster and they want to win games. Their reported interest in Colts running back Jonathan Taylor strongly suggests that, if the Packers could get Taylor, the support for Jacobs would immediately evaporate.

Here’s the basic reality. If the Packers were to cut Jacobs, would anyone sign him? The Chiefs took plenty of heat last week for adding kicker Justin Tucker to the practice squad; they may not be inclined to give another second chance to a player whose arrival would compound the negative reaction to Tucker’s arrival.

The reality, in every sport at every level, is obvious. If the player has enough skill and ability, someone will find a way to justify behavior that shouldn’t be tolerated by an industry that relies so heavily on support from the public, both directly through ticket sales and TV ratings and indirectly through taxpayer funding for stadium projects.

Still, Jacobs has done himself no favors by adopting a posture of defiance. He did something no man should ever do. He’s lucky he’s good enough at football to not lose his job over it.

If he was at the bottom of the depth chart in Green Bay, he would have been gone a long time ago.

His implausible explanation, which accused Prime Video of not showing enough of the celebration and attacked the media for interpreting the images with their lyin' eyes, was contradicted by the full video of the event.

That's not the way to play this. His no-contest plea is equivalent to saying, "I did it." He should be contrite. He should be apologetic. He should be seeking redemption.

The Packers continue to stand behind him, primarily because he's the best running back on the roster and they want to win games. Their reported interest in Colts running back Jonathan Taylor strongly suggests that, if the Packers could get Taylor, the support for Jacobs would immediately evaporate.

Here's the basic reality. If the Packers were to cut Jacobs, would anyone sign him? The Chiefs took plenty of heat last week for adding kicker Justin Tucker to the practice squad; they may not be inclined to give another second chance to a player whose arrival would compound the negative reaction to Tucker's arrival.

The reality, in every sport at every level, is obvious. If the player has enough skill and ability, someone will find a way to justify behavior that shouldn't be tolerated by an industry that relies so heavily on support from the public, both directly through ticket sales and TV ratings and indirectly through taxpayer funding for stadium projects.

Still, Jacobs has done himself no favors by adopting a posture of defiance. He did something no man should ever do. He's lucky he's good enough at football to not lose his job over it.

If he was at the bottom of the depth chart in Green Bay, he would have been gone a long time ago.

Meghan Markle and Prince Harry have been making headlines for a number of reasons as of late but, most notably, the pair have been drawing attention for their recent move. A few months ago, it was reported that the royal couple were planning to move back to the United Kingdom with their two children. The pair eventually did just that and reportedly got there on a private jet that apparently cost them a pretty penny to use. Now, it would seem that while back in the UK, Harry and Markle have just relocated again.

Since being back in the UK, the Duke and Duchess of Sussex – along with children Prince Harry and Princess Lilibet – have reportedly been living with friends in the Cotswolds. More specifically, the brood had taken up residence in what’s been referred to as the “main house” on the property. According to a report from Daily Mail, however, the family is now moving from that house to what’s simply described as a “property on the estate.” Reps for the royals have not provided any concrete information on the matter.

Recent rumors have suggested that Markle and Harry have been searching for a new abode in the British countryside. However, the state of that search remains unclear, given their current stay with their friends. As for this latest move, it purportedly took place while Archie and Lilibet were on their half-term vacation from school.

Why Geography Always Seems To Be A Problem For Prince Harry And Meghan Markle

This latest move comes amid questions about the Duke and Duchess’ decision to leave the United States in the first place. Prince Harry and Meghan Markle initially moved to California in March 2020, only months after stepping back as senior members of the British Royal Family. At that point, they stayed in a house owned by filmmaker Tyler Perry and, by July, they took up permanent residence in Montecito. Around 2023, it was rumored that the two were considering a move to Malibu, but that didn’t come to fruition. It was also in 2023 that the couple formally relinquished their UK home, Frogmore Cottage.

Reports of the Duke and Duchess’ intention to move back to the UK began to surface in August 2026. Shortly after that came the reports of the pair using the private jet to get their family to Harry’s home country. Per numbers that were shared, the jet reportedly cost $12,000 per hour for the 10-hour flight, which apparently brought the grand total of the trip to $120,000. The couple’s exact reasons for returning to the UK remain unknown, though it’s been speculated that the health of Harry’s father, King Charles III, was a contributing factor.

The royals may be back in the UK now, but it’s already been reported that they may return to the US, at least for visits, very soon. Sources allege that Meghan Markle and Prince Harry could be back for the holiday season, considering Markle’s mom, Doria Ragland, still lives in the states. That piece of speculation also came with the claim that Harry and Markle wouldn’t be celebrating with the Royal Family. If any festivities take place in the UK, though, it seems the Duke and Duchess now have a new spot in which they can celebrate.

Filing: Amazon’s previously announced layoffs include 95 jobs in Washington state

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

by Todd Bishop on Nov 3, 2026 at 6:11 pmNovember 3, 2026 at 6:13 pm

A notice made public Tuesday evening, disclosing about 95 Amazon job cuts in Washington state, is part of the company’s previously reported layoffs in its retail business, not a new round of reductions, a spokesperson confirmed.

The filing lists software engineers, product managers and program managers among the affected employees. Most are based in Amazon’s Seattle offices, but others work in Bellevue and Redmond, at warehouses in Sumner and Pasco, or remotely.

Reuters reported in early October that Amazon was laying off fewer than 1,000 corporate employees, mainly in its Stores unit, which runs the company’s e-commerce business. Amazon confirmed to GeekWire at the time that “hundreds” of Stores employees were affected. The notice covers only employees based in Washington state.

“We’ve adjusted parts of our Stores business because we believe this structure will better enable us to deliver on our priorities,” Amazon said in a statement at the time.

Most affected employees were notified in October, including 43 on or about Oct. 7, when the Stores cuts were first reported. Others received notice in September or as early as July 3, according to the filing.

The federal Worker Adjustment and Retraining Notification (WARN) Act requires large employers to give workers and the government advance notice of mass layoffs. Washington’s version of the WARN Act generally requires 60 days’ notice when a covered employer eliminates 50 or more jobs within 30 days across its Washington operations, excluding part-time employees.

Amazon has made a series of smaller filings under the state law in the past year, including notices covering 121 jobs in August, 57 tech jobs earlier in the summer and 84 jobs last December.

The latest notice covers employees at 17 Amazon offices and warehouses in Seattle, Bellevue, Redmond, Sumner and Pasco, plus 15 remote workers in the state.

The list also includes risk managers, several directors and other senior employees. Employees who land other jobs inside Amazon before their separation dates won’t be laid off, the notice says.

The Stores cuts were much smaller than Amazon’s major corporate cutbacks since last fall. The company eliminated about 14,000 corporate roles globally in October 2025, including 2,303 in Washington, followed by another round in January that included 2,198 Washington employees.

Go hands-on with new launches the same week they’re announced, and hear directly from the engineers who built them. Experience the depth you won’t find anywhere else.

Republican Byron Donalds wins governor's race in Florida

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

President Donald Trump listens as Republican Rep. Byron Donalds, speaks at a campaign rally in Daytona Beach, Fla., Friday. Donalds won his election and is set to become the state's first Black governor. Jacquelyn Martin/AP hide caption

Midterms coverage: Live results | What to know | Stories around the countryStay connected: NPR Politics Podcast | NPR Politics newsletter | NPR app

Republican Byron Donalds beat Democrat David Jolly in Florida's closely watched governor's race, according to a race call by the Associated Press.

Donalds, who will become Florida's first Black governor, won the race with the backing of President Donald Trump and a sizable campaign finance war chest. He clinched the governor's race against the national backdrop of Republicans growing unhappy with Trump over gas prices and the war with Iran.

Donalds stumped on affordability issues - inflation, housing and insurance - and promised to continue the conservative policies of out-going Republican Gov. Ron DeSantis, who remains popular in Florida and joined Donalds during the campaign.

Several polls showed a close race favoring Donalds through much of the campaign in a state that has some 1.5 million more registered Republicans than Democrats, and 3.3 million unaffiliated voters up for grabs.

Donalds, 48, who spent most of his career in the finance industry, has served two terms in Congress and two terms in the Florida House. Seen as a rising star in the Republican party, he started as a Democrat and left the party in 2010. Donalds was an early Trump supporter in the 2024 election, and Trump returned the favor by backing him. Donalds rallied with Trump just days before the election.

Polls taken earlier this year showed Trump was less popular in Florida than DeSantis, and in September, Donalds appeared to distance himself from Trump by taking prominent displays of Trump's name off his campaign website.

Jolly campaigned on a "believe in better" slogan, zeroing in on affordability issues such as housing, inflation and gas prices and the boom in AI data centers. And on the trail, he called Donalds "data center Donalds" for taking campaign contributions from industry players. Donalds introduced legislation this summer that would require data centers to generate their own power and water.

Jolly, 54, served in Congress as a Republican before leaving the party in 2018 during President Donald Trump's first term. He changed his registration to no-party affiliation and then switched to Democrat last year. He has spent most of his career as a lawyer, lobbyist and television political analyst.

Rhode Island Voters Back $120M Bond Measure to Support Affordable Housing

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Rhode Island voters are projected to pass a ballot initiative to support increased housing through a massive bond issuance.

Rhode Island's Question 2 issues $120 million in general-obligation bonds, which could then be used in various costs to increase the supply of affordable housing. The funding would go to pre-development work, new construction, property acquisition, and infrastructure improvements.

At 9 p.m. ET on Tuesday, an hour after polls closed, the measure had 61% support with 84% of the vote reported, according to the state's Board of Elections. The measure needs 50% support to pass.

Of that money, $25 million must go to increasing construction of for-sale homes and $10 million for public housing. The state expects construction on these projects to begin in 2028 and the new housing to come online from 2029 to 2032. That's according to the state's information on the ballot measure, which notes that the total cost of the bond will top out at around $192.5 million with interest.

The National Low Income Housing Coalition estimates that Rhode Island, with a population of about 1.1 million people, is short around 23,000 affordable rental homes for low-income renters. Around 30% the state's renters are considered extremely low income, with an average income of $31,970 for a household of four. According to NLIHC, renters need to make an annual household income of $70,617 to afford a two-bedroom rental home at HUD's Fair Market Rent.

Many prospective buyers in Rhode Island are also being priced out.

Rhode Island's median listing price is $569,900, about $150,000 more than the median national listing price of $419,250.

Housing Works Rhode Island's 2026 Housing Fact Book found that purchasing a home in Rhode Island is out of reach for anyone making the median annual income and that housing costs continue to outpace wages.

Around a third of Rhode Island's homeowners are considered cost-burdened, and spend more than 30% of their income on housing.

In the state's capital city, Providence, less than 4% of homes were considered affordable to middle-income buyers, showing a significant misalignment between available homes and local income level. In fact, Providence ranks among Realtor.com's most misaligned markets when it comes to incomes meeting the cost of housing.

Former Gov. Dan McKee, who made housing reform a major focus of his administration, urged voters to support the bill. In September, he was defeated in the primary by Helena Foulkes, who is running against Republican gubernatorial candidate Aaron Guckian.

Guckian expressed opposition to the measure.

"Before we ask taxpayers to borrow another $120 million, plus interest, I think we owe them an accounting of the housing money we're already spending and what we're getting for it," Guckian wrote in an email to the Providence Journal.

Forget Rush Hour 4, Jackie Chan's Sequel To Hit 142-Minute Thriller Confirms His Best New Action Franchise

Jackie Chan is one of the most beloved martial arts movie stars, and his filmography is remarkable because it transcends genres. Chan is a talented martial artist, but he's also a stuntman. His action scenes are often as much about how he uses the environment as they are about trading blows.

Jackie Chan also has multigenerational appeal and has been a mainstay in action cinema for decades. Fans of 70s kung fu movies may know him best as Wong Fei-Hung in Drunken Master. Chan played Keung in Rumble in the Bronx, and even casual moviegoers know him as Chief Inspector Lee in Rush Hour.

The Rush Hour films are adored for many reasons. Chan teams up with Carter (Chris Tucker), an unpredictable LAPD detective, and the pair have incredible on-screen chemistry. The franchise also stands out among similar series because it's lighthearted and laugh-out-loud funny. Fans of Jackie Chan's stunt work won't be disappointed, and the movies are accomplished comedy action films even if you're less interested in the physical feats.

It's no surprise that Rush Hour fans are waiting for news about its fourth installment. However, the actor has also worked on numerous projects in China, and one of those recent Jackie Chan movies is set to become a franchise itself. It may be grittier and more violent than audiences familiar with Rush Hour are used to. Still, it remains an exceptional action movie, with jaw-dropping parkour, and Chan is as charismatic as ever.

The Shadow's Edge was released in 2025, with a sequel reportedly in the works. The movie introduces us to a skilled group of young thieves. They use a combination of high-flying martial arts and parkour, but they also have far more high-tech tools at their disposal. One member of the group is a professional hacker who uses the police force's own technology against them.

The authorities decide to call in a retired expert officer, Wong Tak-chung (Jackie Chan), to help bring the group down. He assembles a young task force, teaching them surveillance and fieldwork without relying so heavily on technology that could expose them. Tak-chung also discovers that the group has links to a mysterious criminal mastermind known as The Shadow (Tony Leung Ka-fai), who has eluded the police for years.

Jackie Chan's character is a great mentor, but he can still fight, and he's the highlight of several incredible action scenes. One particular brawl in a claustrophobic apartment is a big highlight. Leung's action scenes are also incredible as almost futuristic technology clashes with old-school training.

Without delving into spoilers, The Shadow's Edge does have a satisfying conclusion, but it leaves the door wide open for a sequel. It's too early to say when The Shadow's Edge 2 will release, but production officially began in October 2026.

Jackie Chan has been involved in several newer Western releases, including Karate Kid: Legends. He also starred alongside John Cena in Hidden Strike, which premiered in 2023. Several of Chan's latest movies have debuted in China. We've already covered The Shadow's Edge, and it's arguably a must-watch for fans of Jackie Chan movies, even if you don't enjoy subtitles.

Many of Chan's recent Chinese movies are quite different from what some Western fans may be used to. A Legend premiered in 2024 and deserves a special mention because it's a sequel to The Myth, which was released in 2005. Professor Fang (Jackie Chan) is an archaeologist. Ancient relics are discovered during a glacier excavation, and they are significant because Fang has seen them in his dreams.

A Legend tells a story in the present day and another in Ancient China as Fang learns more about the artifacts. Some action scenes set in Ancient China are particularly impressive, with stylish, large-scale battles and personal sword fights. Jackie Chan also starred in Unexpected Family in 2026. It's a charming comedy, though it's also very different from what general audiences may be used to, as it doesn't feature any martial arts.

What Election Day Is Really Like for NBC News' Steve Kornacki

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

NBC News' Steve Kornacki did not wear his signature khakis to cover the United States’ 2026 midterm elections on Nov. 3. But here's what his typical election night routine looks like.

Steve Kornacki has election night all mapped out.

The NBC News chief data analyst returned to cover the 2026 midterm elections on Nov. 3, appearing on the network's 13-hour marathon in what he described as “livestream history.”

“We’ll go nonstop through the night,” Kornacki wrote on X. “Truly hope you’ll come along for the ride.”

While the 47-year-old opted not to wear his signature khakis for this year's shift—opting for a pair of dark trousers instead—he likely will need plenty of caffeine to sustain him during his all-nighter with the “Big Board.”

“Lots of Diet Coke,” he told GQ in 2020. “I'll drink a ton of Diet Coke. I think that's the main thing. Once we start getting election results, I feed off the energy. There's constant stimulus.”

Despite being a self-proclaimed numbers guy, Kornacki often loses count of how many Diet Cokes he drinks throughout his newscasts. As he put it, “It’s just sort of a constant supply.”

“On a normal day, I'll have a couple,” Kornacki explained. “But on election night, I just keep it nearby and I’m just kind of regularly using it.”

So much so, the soda has become his sole source of sustenance during his around-the-clock coverage.

“I do feel eating slows me down,” he shared. “It will make me sleepy. That’s the thing I have found, is that the fatigue will catch up to me when I start eating.”

That’s why also why the political correspondent doesn’t eat at all on election night.

“I'll try to have something during the day. Maybe it's a bagel or something,” Kornacki continued. “I haven't had breakfast in 28 or 29 years.”

In fact, Kornacki only added another beverage to his rider for this year's shift: Dunkin' coffee. Still, the journalist affectionately known to fans as "Map Daddy" doesn't believe he'll need it all too much.

"Once you start doing it," he said of election coverage in an Axios interview published Nov. 3, "the adrenaline of new results, new material, new information constantly coming in, it carries you."

Scientists from Skoltech, together with colleagues from the Kharkevich Institute for Information Transmission Problems of the Russian Academy of Sciences, Lomonosov Moscow State University and other organizations, conducted a comparative study of the 3D genome organization in neurons derived from induced pluripotent stem cells and in neurons of the adult human brain.
